The primary reading is unchanged: macro MRR@20 of RETENTION_RESULT.json (06P P1–P7, 06D §3). NDCG@10 / HR@10 and their CIs stay as retention_report computes them. No claim, contrast, Holm band, selection, stopping rule or recipe change may use a cell of this table.  **(3) No new computation.** The extractor `metric_table.py` (sha256 `561d758ae2431d2a659e42e310d439f446bbf179dcd6d83f8400c1f3d2e77b57`): - binds the block by its BLOCK_INDEX.json sha256 (VALIDATION: E_CHECK_PASS `block_index_sha256`; TEST: the index the TEST report read); - verifies each copied rows file against the index `sources` sha256; - applies the entry's row_filter (device cell) and declared rule exactly as retention_report.select_rows does; - **copies** the five fields verbatim.  It computes no mean, ratio, CI, bootstrap or rounding. A duplicate or missing grid cell refuses (exit 2, nothing written). Seed means, CIs or any other statistic on these metrics are NOT part of this registration. If ever wanted, they would be POST_VALIDATION_EXPLORATORY (06L-C C5) for VALIDATION, and are not allowed after the TEST look.  **(4) When.** VALIDATION: inside F_validation_read.sh, right after the single §9 read, on the same block (`$R/ANA/metric_table_val_<tag>/`). TEST: inside H `analysis`, right after the TEST report (`$R/ANA/metric_table_test_<tag>/`). It runs once each, outputs are write-once and read-only, and stdout is counts only. A refusal stops only the table, never the read. It is re-run, with the same frozen script, into a new `--out` only after a DL row records the cause.  **(5) Freeze / lock.** ANA_SCRIPT_FREEZE carries `descriptive_metric_table` {script, script_sha256, this DL row} and the script in `scripts`. ALL_METHODS_LOCK copies `descriptive_metric_table`. After the freeze the script never changes: a change means a new freeze. Cause: multiprocessing "spawn" re-imports the main module `v4bench.run` as __mp_main__, and its import-time line `os.environ["CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES"] = ""` (CPU default) blanks the GPU in every worker. code_bench5b is pinned by the ML-1M LOCK, so it is not edited. It runs the same module through runpy under -c with alter_sys=False, so the workers do not re-import it (identical code and arguments; only the entry form differs). The driver was relaunched with PY=wrapper CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0 CUBLAS_WORKSPACE_CONFIG=:4096:8 --gpus "0" --slots-per-gpu 4; the looping logs were kept (*.cudaenv_loop, *.cudaenv_loop2).
